提高儿科医生对 RSV 诊断、治疗 和预防的了解对于优化预防策略至关重要。未来的研究应该解决意识差距和实施障碍。

Awareness, knowledge, and attitudes of pediatricians towards respiratory syncytial virus and current and emerging prophylactic strategies: a cross-sectional survey study from a tertiary care hospital in Turkey

Authors: Naci Yılmaz, Aslınur Özkaya Parlakay, Metin Yigit, Kaan Celebier, Furkan Kalaycı, Orkun Tolunay, Naci YILMAZ, Metin Yiğit, Kaan Çelebier
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) infection is the most common cause of lower respiratory tract infections in children under five years of age and is a significant factor in hospitalization. This study aimed to investigate the knowledge, awareness, and attitudes of pediatricians, including pediatric specialists and pediatric residents, towards RSV infection and both locally available (palivizumab) and newly emerging global prophylaxis options (nirsevimab and maternal vaccination). For this cross-sectional survey study, data were collected via an online survey of 216 pediatricians (including 76 pediatric specialists and 140 pediatric residents) between June 2024 and October 2024. Almost all participants (97.7%) were aware that RSV prophylaxis should be administered to children in high-risk groups to reduce the impact of RSV infection on hospitalization, morbidity, and mortality. However, only 78.7% of the participants were aware of vaccination and monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) for RSV prophylaxis. A total of 165 (76.4%) participants recommended prophylaxis specifically for high-risk infants. Awareness regarding maternal vaccination was also evaluated. In contrast, those who did not vaccinate were mostly unaware of the disease’s frequency and cost and believed that breast milk alone was sufficient to prevent it. Approximately 55% of pediatricians believed that RSV prophylaxis was not being implemented effectively. Approximately 83% of the participants said they would opt for it if they had a child and believed that RSV prophylaxis could effectively impact many people, thereby reducing the infection burden. A total of 113 participants (52.3%) believed that mAbs should be administered to all infants. Notably, pediatric specialists demonstrated significantly higher awareness of prophylaxis methods than pediatric residents ( p = 0.004), alongside variations in clinical symptoms and transmission routes. Our findings suggest that improving pediatricians’ knowledge of RSV diagnosis, treatment, and prophylaxis is essential for optimizing preventive strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study focuses on Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) infection, the most common cause of lower respiratory tract infections in children under five years of age, and investigates the knowledge, awareness, and attitudes of pediatricians towards RSV prophylaxis, including locally available and emerging options.
Evidence base and method
A cross-sectional survey study conducted via an online survey of 216 pediatricians (76 specialists and 140 residents) between June 2024 and October 2024.
Consensus and conclusions
Almost all participants (97.7%) agreed that RSV prophylaxis should be administered to high-risk infants to reduce hospitalization, morbidity, and mortality. Pediatric specialists showed significantly higher awareness of prophylaxis methods than residents (p = 0.004).
Controversies and gaps
Only 78.7% of participants were aware of vaccination and monoclonal antibodies for RSV prophylaxis. Approximately 55% believed prophylaxis was not being implemented effectively, and non-vaccinating participants were often unaware of disease frequency and cost.
Research and practice implications
Improving pediatricians' knowledge of RSV diagnosis, treatment, and prophylaxis is essential for optimizing preventive strategies. Future research should address awareness gaps and implementation barriers.

针对免疫功能低下儿童和家庭接触者的流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 疫苗接种。

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.
作者： Carol M. Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A. Heald-Sargent, Timothy D. Minniear, Victoria A. Statler, Sanya J. Thomas, Kathryn E. Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I. Ardura, Lara A. Danziger-Isakov
摘要中文翻译免疫功能低下的儿科患者,包括造血细胞和实体器官移植受者、接受恶性肿瘤化疗的患者以及接受针对自身免疫或炎症状况的生物反应调节剂的患者,有因 疫苗 可预防的呼吸道病毒感染(如流感、SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) 和 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV))而患上严重疾病的风险。这些儿童面临更高的住院率、重症监护率和 死亡率 率,反映出免疫系统不成熟、免疫记忆缺失和/或环境暴露增加的影响。由于缺乏免疫反应和 疫苗 在免疫功能低下儿童中的疗效的主要数据,针对免疫功能低下儿童的免疫建议很大程度上是从健康儿童和/或免疫功能低下成人中进行的研究推断出来的。普通人群对 疫苗 犹豫的趋势、社区呼吸道病毒感染持续存在的 传播 以及免疫功能低下儿童及其家庭接触者中 疫苗接种 率不理想的情况进一步加剧了这一弱势群体的风险。随着患有免疫功能低下的儿童数量的增加,护理免疫功能低下儿童的初级保健从业者和专科医生必须确保为这些患者及其家庭和社区接触者提供适当的免疫接种。我们将审查支持当前关于 疫苗 剂量、时间安排和配方的指南的可用数据,以预防免疫功能低下儿童的流感、COVID-19 和 RSV 感染。此外,我们强调了关键知识差距和当前研究领域,旨在提高呼吸道病毒 疫苗 免疫原性和优化保护。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 以及流感和 SARS-CoV-2,针对免疫功能低下的儿科患者(例如移植接受者、化疗接受者和使用生物调节剂的患者)及其家庭接触者。核心问题涉及 疫苗 的建议、剂量、时间安排和配方,以预防这些呼吸道病毒感染的严重后果。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有数据的叙述性综合,因为没有报告系统搜索、数据库或荟萃分析方法。由于免疫功能低下儿童的主要数据有限,它根据健康儿童和免疫功能低下成人的研究进行推断。
共识与结论
由于免疫功能障碍和环境暴露,免疫功能低下的儿童因 RSV、流感和 COVID-19 而面临更高的住院率、入住 ICU 和 死亡率 率。目前的 疫苗 指南基于推断证据,缺乏该人群的直接免疫学和疗效数据。
争议与缺口
主要差距包括关于 疫苗 对免疫功能低下儿童的免疫原性和疗效的主要数据不足,再加上 疫苗 犹豫不决、社区 传播 以及该群体及其接触者中 疫苗 率次优。
科研与实践启示
从业人员必须确保及时为免疫功能低下的儿童及其接触者提供 疫苗接种。未来的研究应该解决免疫反应、优化 疫苗 配方并改进针对这一弱势群体的保护策略。

Influenza, COVID-19, and RSV Vaccinations for Immunocompromised Children and Household Contacts.

Authors: Carol M. Kao, Hannah Bahakel, Taylor A. Heald-Sargent, Timothy D. Minniear, Victoria A. Statler, Sanya J. Thomas, Kathryn E. Weakley, Qing Cao, Monica I. Ardura, Lara A. Danziger-Isakov
Original abstract (English)
Immunocompromised pediatric patients, including hematopoietic cell and solid organ transplant recipients, those undergoing chemotherapy for malignancy, and those receiving biologic response modifiers for autoimmune or inflammatory conditions, are at risk for severe disease from vaccine-preventable respiratory viral infections such as influenza, SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), and respiratory syncytial virus (RSV). These children face higher rates of hospitalization, intensive care admissions, and mortality, reflecting contributions from an immature immune system, absence of immunologic memory, and/or increased environmental exposures. Immunization recommendations for immunocompromised children are largely extrapolated from studies conducted among healthy children and/or immunocompromised adults due to a paucity of primary data on immunological responses and vaccine efficacy in immunocompromised children. Trends in vaccine hesitancy in the general population, ongoing transmission of respiratory viral infections in the community, and suboptimal vaccination rates among immunocompromised children and their household contacts further compound the risk for this vulnerable population. As the number of children with immunocompromising conditions expands, it is imperative that primary care practitioners and subspecialists who care for immunocompromised children ensure appropriate immunizations are provided to these patients and their household and community contacts. We will review available data supporting the current guidelines regarding vaccine dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent influenza, COVID-19, and RSV infections in immunocompromised children. Furthermore, we highlight key knowledge gaps and areas of current investigation aimed at improving respiratory viral vaccine immunogenicity and optimize protection.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) alongside influenza and SARS-CoV-2, targeting immunocompromised pediatric patients (e.g., transplant recipients, chemotherapy recipients, and those on biologic modifiers) and their household contacts. The central question concerns vaccine recommendations, dosing, scheduling, and formulations to prevent severe outcomes from these respiratory viral infections.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing data, as no systematic search, database, or meta-analysis methodology is reported. It extrapolates from studies in healthy children and immunocompromised adults due to limited primary data in immunocompromised children.
Consensus and conclusions
Immunocompromised children face higher rates of hospitalization, ICU admission, and mortality from RSV, influenza, and COVID-19 due to immune dysfunction and environmental exposures. Current vaccine guidelines are based on extrapolated evidence, with direct immunological and efficacy data lacking in this population.
Controversies and gaps
Key gaps include insufficient primary data on vaccine immunogenicity and efficacy in immunocompromised children, compounded by vaccine hesitancy, community transmission, and suboptimal vaccination rates among this group and their contacts.
Research and practice implications
Practitioners must ensure timely vaccinations for immunocompromised children and their contacts. Future research should address immunological responses, optimize vaccine formulations, and improve protection strategies for this vulnerable population.

[心血管综合预防:疫苗接种在患有心血管疾病的老年人中的作用。专家共识文件]。

[Comprehensive cardiovascular prevention: the role of vaccination in older adults with cardiovascular disease. Expert consensus document].
作者： C Bonanad, R Alfaro, Á Morán Bayón, M Cainzos-Achirica, A García-Lledó, S Raposeiras-Roubín, D Vivas, R Freixa-Pamias, J Mora, M A Martín-Toro
摘要中文翻译简介:呼吸道和全身感染可通过增加炎症、动脉粥样硬化斑块不稳定和血栓前状态而引发心血管事件。鉴于这种情况,疫苗接种 是心血管疾病(CVD)患者的相关预防工具。目的:回顾 疫苗接种 对 CVD 患者影响的现有证据,并提出提高 疫苗接种 在临床实践中覆盖率的建议。内容:这一专家共识是通过结构化叙述性审查和小组讨论形成的,综合了 疫苗接种 对抗流感、肺炎球菌疾病、呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染、COVID-19 和 CVD 患者带状疱疹的证据。流感 疫苗 提供了减少心血管事件的最有力证据,特别是在急性发作后和高危患者中。对于 疫苗接种 针对肺炎球菌疾病、COVID-19、RSV 感染和带状疱疹的治疗,尽管直接心血管证据较为有限,但感染和住院治疗的减少支持了其对综合预防方法的建议。结论:疫苗接种 是综合心血管预防方法的一部分。在心脏病学和初级保健之间的共享途径以及公共卫生服务的支持下,扩大覆盖范围可以减少感染并发症,减少心血管失代偿,并获得更好的临床结果。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 疫苗接种 在患有心血管疾病 (CVD) 的老年人中的作用,探讨其通过减少感染引发的心血管事件在综合心血管预防中的作用。
证据基础与方法
该专家共识是通过结构化叙述审查和小组讨论形成的,综合了 疫苗接种 针对 CVD 患者的流感、肺炎球菌疾病、RSV、COVID-19 和带状疱疹的证据。
共识与结论
疫苗接种 是综合心血管预防方法的一部分,流感 疫苗 显示出减少心血管事件的最有力证据,特别是在急性发作后和高危患者中。
争议与缺口
尽管感染和住院治疗的减少支持了他们的建议,但肺炎球菌疾病、COVID-19、RSV 和带状疱疹 疫苗接种 的直接心血管证据更为有限。
科研与实践启示
通过心脏病学、初级保健和公共卫生服务之间的共享途径改善 疫苗接种 覆盖范围可以减少感染并发症、心血管代偿失调并改善临床结果。

[Comprehensive cardiovascular prevention: the role of vaccination in older adults with cardiovascular disease. Expert consensus document].

Authors: C Bonanad, R Alfaro, Á Morán Bayón, M Cainzos-Achirica, A García-Lledó, S Raposeiras-Roubín, D Vivas, R Freixa-Pamias, J Mora, M A Martín-Toro
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: Respiratory and systemic infections can trigger cardiovascular events by increasing inflammation, atherosclerotic plaque instability, and a prothrombotic state. Given this situation, vaccination is a relevant preventive tool in patients with cardiovascular disease (CVD). OBJECTIVE: To review the available evidence on the impact of vaccination in patients with CVD and to propose recommendations to improve vaccination coverage in clinical practice. CONTENT: This expert consensus, developed through a structured narrative review and panel discussion rounds, synthesizes the evidence on vaccination against influenza, pneumococcal disease, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection, COVID-19, and herpes zoster in patients with CVD. The influenza vaccine presents the strongest evidence for reducing cardiovascular events, especially after acute episodes and in high-risk patients. For vaccination against pneumococcal disease, COVID-19, RSV infection, and herpes zoster, although direct cardiovascular evidence is more limited, the reduction in infections and hospitalizations supports its recommendation for a comprehensive preventive approach. CONCLUSIONS: Vaccination is part of a comprehensive cardiovascular prevention approach. Improved coverage, supported by shared pathways between cardiology and primary care, along with public health services, can result in fewer infectious complications, fewer cardiovascular decompensations, and better clinical outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccination in older adults with cardiovascular disease (CVD), examining its role in comprehensive cardiovascular prevention by reducing infection-triggered cardiovascular events.
Evidence base and method
This expert consensus was developed through a structured narrative review and panel discussion rounds, synthesizing evidence on vaccination against influenza, pneumococcal disease, RSV, COVID-19, and herpes zoster in patients with CVD.
Consensus and conclusions
Vaccination is part of a comprehensive cardiovascular prevention approach, with the influenza vaccine showing the strongest evidence for reducing cardiovascular events, especially after acute episodes and in high-risk patients.
Controversies and gaps
Direct cardiovascular evidence for pneumococcal disease, COVID-19, RSV, and herpes zoster vaccinations is more limited, though infection and hospitalization reduction supports their recommendation.
Research and practice implications
Improved vaccination coverage through shared pathways between cardiology, primary care, and public health services can reduce infectious complications, cardiovascular decompensations, and improve clinical outcomes.

内源性大麻素系统调节呼吸系统疾病:治疗视角。

Modulation of Respiratory Diseases by the Endocannabinoid System: A Therapeutic Perspective.
作者： Shiyuan Qin, Siyang Shen, Xiaoqin Chen, Pengfei Yi, Bendong Fu, Luyuan Peng
摘要中文翻译呼吸系统疾病是全球主要的健康负担,其特点是高 患病率 和显着的发病率和 死亡率。呼吸道直接且持续地暴露于外部环境,使其特别容易受到病原体、污染物和其他有害物质的侵害。这种暴露通常会引发过度的炎症反应,损害气血屏障的完整性,导致气体交换受损、缺氧和呼吸衰竭。因此,调节肺部炎症和增强屏障功能是关键的治疗目标。内源性大麻素系统是一种普遍存在的信号网络,由大麻素受体、内源性配体和代谢酶组成,已成为这些过程的关键调节剂。本综述总结了 ECS 在主要呼吸系统疾病中的作用,包括哮喘、肺纤维化、呼吸道合胞病毒 感染和急性肺损伤。证据强调了通过受体特异性配体和内源性大麻素降解酶抑制剂等策略靶向 ECS 的治疗潜力。然而,ECS 调制的上下文相关性质需要精确的干预。未来的努力应集中于开发选择性疗法并在临床环境中验证其疗效,将 ECS 定位为创新呼吸系统疾病管理的复杂目标。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 感染作为一种主要呼吸道疾病,探讨调节内源性大麻素系统 (ECS) 解决肺部炎症和屏障功能障碍的治疗潜力。
证据基础与方法
该综述总结了 ECS 在主要呼吸道疾病(包括 RSV 感染)中的作用,但没有具体说明数据库、搜索日期或研究计数。
共识与结论
一致认为,通过受体特异性配体和内源性大麻素降解酶抑制剂靶向 ECS 具有治疗 RSV 感染和其他呼吸道疾病的潜力。
争议与缺口
ECS 调节的背景依赖性需要精确的干预,并且缺乏在临床环境中针对 RSV 特异性 治疗 进行验证的选择性治疗方法。
科研与实践启示
未来的努力应集中于开发选择性 ECS 靶向疗法并验证其在临床环境中的疗效,以创新 RSV 疾病管理。

Modulation of Respiratory Diseases by the Endocannabinoid System: A Therapeutic Perspective.

Authors: Shiyuan Qin, Siyang Shen, Xiaoqin Chen, Pengfei Yi, Bendong Fu, Luyuan Peng
Original abstract (English)
Respiratory system diseases represent a major global health burden, characterized by high prevalence and significant morbidity and mortality. The respiratory tract's direct and continuous exposure to the external environment makes it particularly vulnerable to pathogens, pollutants and other injurious agents. This exposure often triggers excessive inflammatory responses and compromises the integrity of the air-blood barrier, leading to impaired gas exchange, hypoxia and respiratory failure. Therefore, modulating pulmonary inflammation and enhancing barrier function are critical therapeutic objectives. The endocannabinoid system, a ubiquitous signalling network comprising cannabinoid receptors, endogenous ligands and metabolic enzymes, has emerged as a crucial modulator of these processes. This review summarizes the role of the ECS in major respiratory diseases, including asthma, pulmonary fibrosis, respiratory syncytial virus infection and acute lung injury. The evidence highlights the therapeutic potential of targeting the ECS through strategies such as receptor-specific ligands and inhibitors of endocannabinoid-degrading enzymes. However, the context-dependent nature of ECS modulation necessitates precise intervention. Future efforts should focus on developing selective therapeutics and validating their efficacy in clinical settings, positioning the ECS as a sophisticated target for innovative respiratory disease management.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infection as a major respiratory disease, examining the therapeutic potential of modulating the endocannabinoid system (ECS) to address pulmonary inflammation and barrier dysfunction.
Evidence base and method
The review summarizes the role of the ECS in major respiratory diseases, including RSV infection, but does not specify databases, search dates, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The consensus is that targeting the ECS through receptor-specific ligands and inhibitors of endocannabinoid-degrading enzymes holds therapeutic potential for RSV infection and other respiratory diseases.
Controversies and gaps
The context-dependent nature of ECS modulation necessitates precise intervention, and there is a lack of selective therapeutics validated in clinical settings for RSV-specific treatment.
Research and practice implications
Future efforts should focus on developing selective ECS-targeted therapeutics and validating their efficacy in clinical settings to innovate RSV disease management.

儿科侵袭性 A 组链球菌病:早期识别、快速恶化和急诊室管理

Pediatric Invasive Group A Streptococcal Disease: Early Recognition, Rapid Deterioration, and Emergency Department Management
作者： Mayar M. Aziz, Israa Magdy Ata, Mohammed A. Mahmoud, Sarah Fakhiraldeen, AHMED ABDELMONEM, Seleen Abu Alhmam, Haneen, Mohammed Alsabri
摘要中文翻译摘要 综述目的侵袭性 A 族链球菌感染 (iGAS) 是儿童中罕见但危及生命的疾病。Following the COVID-19 pandemic,several countries have reported a resurgence of pediatric iGAS cases with increased disease severity and higher rates of intensive care admission.这篇叙述性综述为急诊医生提供了关于后新冠时代儿童 iGAS 的早期识别、诊断方法和管理的实用临床概述。它综合了当前关于儿科 iGAS 的流行病学研究、临床证据和指南建议,重点是早期预警信号、诊断策略和时间紧迫的急诊科管理。Recent Findings Recent epidemiologic data demonstrate a marked rise in pediatric iGAS 发病率 since 2022. Key risk factors include age under five years,viral respiratory coinfections (particularly influenza and 呼吸道合胞病毒),varicella infection,and the circulation of virulent emm serotypes. Clinical presentations range from bacteremia and pneumonia to severe toxin-mediated syndromes such as streptococcal toxic shock syndrome and necrotizing fasciitis.早期预警信号包括临床迅速恶化、与检查结果不成比例的剧烈疼痛、胃肠道症状、精神状态改变和血流动力学不稳定。及时的诊断评估,包括实验室检测、微生物培养和靶向成像,至关重要。紧急处理需要脓毒症快速复苏、早期联合抗生素治疗(对于脓毒症,经验性β-内酰胺治疗;对于疑似iGAS,经验性抗生素治疗应覆盖金黄色葡萄球菌和iGAS,使用β-内酰胺加克林霉素发挥抗毒素作用;对于由β-内酰胺敏感的iGAS引起的确诊iGAS,治疗应为β-内酰胺治疗加克林霉素和经验性 治疗 后,应根据培养结果和临床表现调整抗生素治疗方案,并在怀疑坏死性感染时紧急外科会诊。对于毒素介导的疾病,可以考虑辅助治疗,例如静脉注射免疫球蛋白。摘要 大流行后儿科 iGAS 的复苏凸显了在紧急情况下提高临床警惕性的必要性。及早识别危险信号并迅速启动适当的管理对于降低受影响儿童的发病率和 死亡率 至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注儿童中的侵袭性 A 族链球菌感染 (iGAS),检查其早期识别、诊断方法和后新冠时代的急诊科管理。核心问题是如何通过提高临床警惕性和快速干预来改善结果。
证据基础与方法
这是一篇叙述性综述,综合了当前关于儿科 iGAS 的流行病学研究、临床证据和指南建议。它强调早期预警信号、诊断策略和时间紧迫的急诊部门管理。
共识与结论
人们一致认为,大流行后儿科 iGAS 会卷土重来,且严重程度更高,ICU 入住率更高。主要结论包括早期识别危险信号、快速诊断评估以及及时启动适当管理以降低发病率和 死亡率 的重要性。
争议与缺口
该审查没有明确提及争议或具体的证据差距,但强调由于病例的死灰复燃和严重程度的增加,需要提高临床警惕性。
科研与实践启示
其含义包括需要早期识别警告信号、快速脓毒症复苏、早期联合抗生素治疗、对疑似坏死性感染进行紧急外科会诊,以及在毒素介导的疾病中考虑静脉注射免疫球蛋白等辅助治疗。

Pediatric Invasive Group A Streptococcal Disease: Early Recognition, Rapid Deterioration, and Emergency Department Management

Authors: Mayar M. Aziz, Israa Magdy Ata, Mohammed A. Mahmoud, Sarah Fakhiraldeen, AHMED ABDELMONEM, Seleen Abu Alhmam, Haneen, Mohammed Alsabri
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Purpose of Review Invasive group A streptococcal infections (iGAS) are uncommon but life-threatening conditions in children. Following the COVID-19 pandemic, several countries have reported a resurgence of pediatric iGAS cases with increased disease severity and higher rates of intensive care admission. This narrative review provides a practical clinical overview for emergency physicians on the early recognition, diagnostic approach, and management of pediatric iGAS in the post-COVID era. It synthesizes current epidemiological studies, clinical evidence, and guideline recommendations on pediatric iGAS, with emphasis on early warning signs, diagnostic strategies, and time-critical emergency department management. Recent Findings Recent epidemiologic data demonstrate a marked rise in pediatric iGAS incidence since 2022. Key risk factors include age under five years, viral respiratory coinfections (particularly influenza and respiratory syncytial virus), varicella infection, and the circulation of virulent emm serotypes. Clinical presentations range from bacteremia and pneumonia to severe toxin-mediated syndromes such as streptococcal toxic shock syndrome and necrotizing fasciitis. Early warning signs include rapid clinical deterioration, severe pain out of proportion to examination findings, gastrointestinal symptoms, altered mental status, and hemodynamic instability. Prompt diagnostic evaluation, including laboratory testing, microbiologic cultures, and targeted imaging, is essential. Emergency management requires rapid sepsis resuscitation, early combination antibiotic therapy (for sepsis, empirical β-lactam therapy; for suspected iGAS, empirical antibiotic therapy should provide coverage for both Staphylococcus aureus and iGAS using a β-lactam plus clindamycin for its antitoxin effects; for confirmed iGAS caused by β-lactam-susceptible iGAS, treatment should be β-lactam therapy plus clindamycin and vancomycin. Following empirical treatment, antibiotic regimens should be tailored according to culture results and the clinical presentation), and urgent surgical consultation when necrotizing infection is suspected. Adjunctive therapies such as intravenous immunoglobulin may be considered in toxin-mediated disease. Summary The post-pandemic resurgence of pediatric iGAS highlights the need for heightened clinical vigilance in emergency settings. Early recognition of red flags, and rapid initiation of appropriate management are critical to reducing morbidity and mortality in affected children.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on invasive group A streptococcal infections (iGAS) in children, examining their early recognition, diagnostic approach, and emergency department management in the post-COVID era. The central question is how to improve outcomes through heightened clinical vigilance and rapid intervention.
Evidence base and method
This is a narrative review that synthesizes current epidemiological studies, clinical evidence, and guideline recommendations on pediatric iGAS. It emphasizes early warning signs, diagnostic strategies, and time-critical emergency department management.
Consensus and conclusions
There is consensus on the post-pandemic resurgence of pediatric iGAS with increased severity and higher ICU admission rates. Key conclusions include the importance of early recognition of red flags, rapid diagnostic evaluation, and prompt initiation of appropriate management to reduce morbidity and mortality.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not explicitly mention controversies or specific evidence gaps, but it highlights the need for heightened clinical vigilance due to the resurgence of cases and increased severity.
Research and practice implications
Implications include the need for early recognition of warning signs, rapid sepsis resuscitation, early combination antibiotic therapy, urgent surgical consultation for suspected necrotizing infections, and consideration of adjunctive therapies like intravenous immunoglobulin in toxin-mediated disease.

嗜酸性粒细胞在小儿急性喘息治疗中的作用:范围审查

The role of eosinophils in the management of acute paediatric wheeze: a scoping review
作者： Hannah Norman-Bruce, David J. McCleary, Q S Gan, Helen Groves, Thomas Waterfield, P. Jane McDowell
摘要中文翻译急性喘息是紧急护理中最常见的儿科症状之一。尽管血液嗜酸性粒细胞 (BEO) 是稳定型哮喘 2 型炎症的既定生物标志物,但其在指导急性治疗(特别是全身性皮质类固醇使用)中的作用仍不清楚。本次综述的目的是绘制和综合关于儿童急性喘息加重期间采集的 BEO 样本的测量和临床效用的现有证据。范围界定审查是根据乔安娜·布里格斯研究所的方法和范围界定审查的系统审查和元分析扩展的首选报告项目 (PRISMA-ScR) 指南进行的。对 Medline、Embase、Cochrane Library 和 Web of Science 进行了搜索,查找过去 20 年发表的涉及 16 岁以下儿童出现严重喘息并接受嗜酸性粒细胞检测的研究。包括前瞻性和回顾性研究。根据嗜酸性粒细胞测量方法、报告值、截止值以及与特征和 治疗 反应的关联,描述性地总结了数据。八项异质性研究 (n = 855) 符合纳入标准,其中包括五项观察性或回顾性研究以及三项随机对照试验。BEO 报告差异很大,单位不一致、截止值和采样时间描述不明确。没有研究使用即时检测。在各项研究中,嗜酸性粒细胞动态因年龄、喘息表型、疾病严重程度和病毒病因而异,无法进行有意义的比较。支持嗜酸性粒细胞指导使用皮质类固醇的证据有限。两项研究表明,对于患有鼻病毒相关喘息和较高 BEO 的儿童,全身性皮质类固醇治疗可改善预后,而在 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 相关喘息中观察到较低的 BEO。缺乏描述儿童喘息急性加重期间 BEO 的高质量数据。目前支持嗜酸性粒细胞指导护理的证据主要来自稳定的门诊患者 队列研究,可能无法直接适用于急性情况。需要在急性表现期间对 BEO 进行标准化抽样的前瞻性研究,以指导基于精确度的皮质类固醇的使用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次范围审查探讨了血液嗜酸性粒细胞 (BEO) 在治疗儿童急性喘息中的作用,重点关注其在 16 岁以下儿童病情加重期间的测量和临床效用。
证据基础与方法
根据 Joanna Briggs Institute 方法和 PRISMA-ScR 指南进行范围界定审查,搜索 Medline、Embase、Cochrane Library 和 Web of Science 来查找过去 20 年发表的研究。纳入了八项异质性研究(n = 855),其中包括五项观察/回顾性研究和三项随机对照试验。
共识与结论
BEO 报告差异很大,单位不一致、截止值和采样时间描述不明确。没有研究使用即时检测。嗜酸性粒细胞动态因年龄、喘息表型、疾病严重程度和病毒病因而异,因此无法进行有意义的比较。支持嗜酸性粒细胞指导使用皮质类固醇的证据有限。
争议与缺口
缺乏描述儿童喘息急性加重期间 BEO 的高质量数据。目前的证据主要来自稳定的门诊患者 队列研究,可能无法直接适用于急性情况。研究设计和报告的异质性限制了可比性。
科研与实践启示
需要在急性表现期间对 BEO 进行标准化抽样的前瞻性研究,以指导基于精确度的皮质类固醇的使用。两项研究表明,对于患有鼻病毒相关喘息和较高 BEO 的儿童,全身性皮质类固醇可以改善预后,而在 RSV 相关喘息中观察到较低的 BEO。

The role of eosinophils in the management of acute paediatric wheeze: a scoping review

Authors: Hannah Norman-Bruce, David J. McCleary, Q S Gan, Helen Groves, Thomas Waterfield, P. Jane McDowell
Original abstract (English)
Acute wheeze is one of the most common paediatric presentations to emergency care. Although blood eosinophils (BEO) are an established biomarker of type 2 inflammation in stable asthma, their role in guiding acute management—particularly systemic corticosteroid use—remains unclear. The aim of this review was to map and synthesise the existing evidence on the measurement and clinical utility of BEO sampled during acute wheeze exacerbations in children. A scoping review was conducted in accordance with Joanna Briggs Institute methodology and Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analysis extension for scoping reviews (PRISMA-ScR) guidelines. Medline, Embase, Cochrane Library and Web of Science were searched for studies published in the last 20 years involving children under 16 years presenting acutely with wheeze and undergoing eosinophil testing. Prospective and retrospective studies were included. Data were summarised descriptively according to eosinophil measurement methods, reported values, cut-offs and associations with characteristics and treatment response. Eight heterogeneous studies (n = 855) met inclusion criteria, comprising five observational or retrospective studies and three randomised controlled trials. BEO reporting varied widely, with inconsistent units, cut-offs and poorly described sampling timing. No study utilised point-of-care testing. Across studies, eosinophil dynamics differed by age, wheeze phenotype, disease severity and viral aetiology, preventing meaningful comparison. Evidence supporting eosinophil-guided corticosteroid use was limited. Two studies demonstrated improved outcomes with systemic corticosteroids in children with rhinovirus-associated wheeze and higher BEO, while lower BEO were observed in respiratory syncytial virus (RSV)-associated wheeze. There is a paucity of high-quality data describing BEO during acute wheeze exacerbations in children. Current evidence underpinning eosinophil-guided care is largely derived from stable outpatient cohorts and may not be directly applicable to acute settings. Prospective studies with standardised sampling of BEO during acute presentations are needed to inform precision-based corticosteroid use.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This scoping review examines the role of blood eosinophils (BEO) in managing acute paediatric wheeze, focusing on their measurement and clinical utility during exacerbations in children under 16 years.
Evidence base and method
A scoping review following Joanna Briggs Institute methodology and PRISMA-ScR guidelines, searching Medline, Embase, Cochrane Library and Web of Science for studies published in the last 20 years. Eight heterogeneous studies (n = 855) were included, comprising five observational/retrospective studies and three randomised controlled trials.
Consensus and conclusions
BEO reporting varied widely with inconsistent units, cut-offs and poorly described sampling timing. No study used point-of-care testing. Eosinophil dynamics differed by age, wheeze phenotype, disease severity and viral aetiology, preventing meaningful comparison. Evidence supporting eosinophil-guided corticosteroid use was limited.
Controversies and gaps
There is a paucity of high-quality data describing BEO during acute wheeze exacerbations in children. Current evidence is largely derived from stable outpatient cohorts and may not be directly applicable to acute settings. Heterogeneity in study design and reporting limits comparability.
Research and practice implications
Prospective studies with standardised sampling of BEO during acute presentations are needed to inform precision-based corticosteroid use. Two studies suggested improved outcomes with systemic corticosteroids in children with rhinovirus-associated wheeze and higher BEO, while lower BEO were observed in RSV-associated wheeze.

RSV 免疫开发中的公平设计

Equity-by-design in RSV immunisation development
作者： Jonne Terstappen
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV),这是一种感染几乎所有两岁儿童的病原体,会导致婴儿,特别是早产儿和患有合并症的婴儿严重下呼吸道感染。核心问题涉及低收入和中低收入国家 (LMIC) 婴儿 RSV 免疫接种方面的不平等问题。
证据基础与方法
本论文使用了跨转化领域的五种研究类型:公共卫生、基础、临床前、临床和实施研究。它包括对 RSV 疫苗/mAb 格局的回顾(5 种已上市产品,截至 2024 年 6 月有 30 种正在开发中)、与南非索韦托的医护人员共同创建研讨会、干血 RSV 中和抗体测定的开发和验证,以及鼻内帕利珠单抗滴剂的独立于行业的开发。
共识与结论
尼塞韦单抗 对 RSV 住院治疗的有效性超过 80%,但尽管中低收入国家承受着最大的疾病负担,但获得途径仍然集中在高收入国家。干血测定(R2 > 0.95)成功地替代了生物分析中的血清,并被转移到加纳实验室。在 2b 期随机对照试验中,鼻内帕利珠单抗是安全的,但不能显着预防 RSV 感染。RSM01对突变体具有更高的抵抗力,抵抗力通常会降低病毒的适应性。
争议与缺口
由于后勤和财务障碍以及安全性和有效性数据方面的差距,中低收入国家 RSV 预防的实施滞后。尽管鼻内单克隆抗体减少了症状感染,但病毒滴度并未降低,这表明它们不太可能是一种可行的、具有成本效益的保护策略。目前的 RSV 产品开发遵循专为高收入环境设计的“滴流”方法。
科研与实践启示
提出了公平设计框架,并提出了四项建议:与中低收入国家合作伙伴的科学共同所有权、对当地能力的运营投资、早期开发的经济可行性以及与社区优先事项的社会融合。获得药物的机制(自愿许可、技术转让)与世卫组织资格预审和免疫联盟等资助机制相结合,对于负担得起的中低收入国家获得药物至关重要。

Equity-by-design in RSV immunisation development

Authors: Jonne Terstappen
Original abstract (English)
Background Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) infects nearly all children by age two, with reinfections common throughout life. While most infections cause mild upper respiratory symptoms, RSV can cause severe, potentially fatal lower respiratory tract infections in infants, particularly premature babies and those with comorbidities, with outcomes often worse in low- and lower-middle-income countries (LMICs) lacking adequate healthcare access. Until recently, palivizumab (a short-acting monoclonal antibody, mAb) was the only approved preventive option, but its high cost limited use to high-risk infants. Nirsevimab (2022) became the first long-acting mAb approved for all infants, followed by a maternal RSV vaccine, and clesrovimab (2025). The WHO now recommends RSV prevention via maternal vaccination or mAbs, but LMIC implementation lags due to logistical and financial barriers and gaps in safety and efficacy data. This thesis uses five research types across the translational spectrum to address inequities in infant RSV immunization access in LMICs: public health, basic, preclinical, clinical, and implementation research. Key Findings: Chapter 2 reviews the RSV vaccine/mAb landscape (as of June 2024: 5 marketed products, 30 in development), noting nirsevimab's >80% effectiveness against RSV hospitalization, while access remains concentrated in high-income countries despite LMICs bearing the greatest disease burden. Chapter 3 argues that access-to-medicine mechanisms (voluntary licensing, technology transfer) combined with WHO prequalification and funding mechanisms like Gavi are essential for affordable LMIC access. Chapter 4 identifies barriers and facilitators for maternal immunization in Soweto, South Africa, through co-creation workshops with healthcare workers, yielding recommendations on education, community awareness, reporting incentives, and improved medical record-keeping. Chapters 5–7 describe developing and validating a dried blood RSV-neutralizing antibody assay (using samples from a Gates MRI RSM01 phase 1 trial), showing dried blood can replace serum in bioanalyses (R2 > 0.95), and successfully transferring this assay to a Ghanaian laboratory, strengthening local research capacity. Chapters 8–9 cover industry-independent development of intranasal palivizumab drops. A phase 2b RCT in late-preterm infants found daily intranasal dosing was safe but did not significantly prevent RSV infection (38.3% vs 23.4% placebo). A controlled human infection model study in adults showed no reduction in viral titer despite a 72% reduction in symptomatic infections, suggesting intranasal mAbs are unlikely to be a viable, cost-effective protection strategy. Chapter 10 evaluates mAb-resistant mutants (MARMs) under suboptimal antibody pressure for palivizumab, nirsevimab, and RSM01, finding RSM01 has a higher resistance barrier and that resistance generally reduces viral fitness, making global dominance of resistant strains unlikely. Discussion The thesis argues that current RSV product development follows a "trickle-down" approach designed for high-income contexts. It proposes an equity-by-design framework built on three lessons (begin with the end in mind, simplify methods for LMIC contexts, embed affordability early) and four recommendations: scientific co-ownership with LMIC partners, operational investment in local capacity, economic feasibility built in from early development, and social integration with community priorities—positioning equity as a foundational principle rather than an afterthought in RSV immunization development.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), a pathogen that infects nearly all children by age two, causing severe lower respiratory tract infections in infants, particularly premature babies and those with comorbidities. The central question addresses inequities in infant RSV immunization access in low- and lower-middle-income countries (LMICs).
Evidence base and method
This dissertation uses five research types across the translational spectrum: public health, basic, preclinical, clinical, and implementation research. It includes a review of the RSV vaccine/mAb landscape (5 marketed products, 30 in development as of June 2024), co-creation workshops with healthcare workers in Soweto, South Africa, development and validation of a dried blood RSV-neutralizing antibody assay, and industry-independent development of intranasal palivizumab drops.
Consensus and conclusions
Nirsevimab shows >80% effectiveness against RSV hospitalization, but access remains concentrated in high-income countries despite LMICs bearing the greatest disease burden. The dried blood assay (R2 > 0.95) successfully replaced serum in bioanalyses and was transferred to a Ghanaian laboratory. Intranasal palivizumab was safe but did not significantly prevent RSV infection in a phase 2b RCT. RSM01 has a higher resistance barrier against mutants, and resistance generally reduces viral fitness.
Controversies and gaps
LMIC implementation of RSV prevention lags due to logistical and financial barriers and gaps in safety and efficacy data. Intranasal mAbs showed no reduction in viral titer despite reducing symptomatic infections, suggesting they are unlikely to be a viable, cost-effective protection strategy. Current RSV product development follows a "trickle-down" approach designed for high-income contexts.
Research and practice implications
An equity-by-design framework is proposed with four recommendations: scientific co-ownership with LMIC partners, operational investment in local capacity, economic feasibility built in from early development, and social integration with community priorities. Access-to-medicine mechanisms (voluntary licensing, technology transfer) combined with WHO prequalification and funding mechanisms like Gavi are essential for affordable LMIC access.

A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

更新 - 塔斯马尼亚的急性呼吸道感染活动 - 塔斯马尼亚卫生部

Update - acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania - Tasmanian Department of Health
新闻简报塔斯马尼亚卫生部发布了截至 2026 年 7 月 19 日急性呼吸道感染活动的最新信息。虽然总体急性呼吸道疾病 (ARI) 活动仍然较低,但正在逐渐增加,但 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 的活动中度且有所增加,与季节性模式一致。相比之下,COVID-19 和流感活动仍然较低,与近年来相比,流感水平低于预期水平。该部门在旺季期间每两周更新一次此信息,并引导公众查看 RespTas 报告以获取详细的 监测 数据。当局强调,了解当前的活动水平有助于个人采取明智的措施来降低 传播 风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-24;截至 2026 年 7 月 19 日星期日的活动数据。
地点与对象
澳大利亚塔斯马尼亚;受急性呼吸道感染影响的普通人群。
事件
塔斯马尼亚卫生部报告的急性呼吸道感染活动的官方更新,包括确认的 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 中度活动。
规模、影响与风险
RSV 活性中等且不断增加;COVID-19 和流感活动仍然较低;总体急性呼吸道疾病活动较低,但逐渐增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
在季节性活动增加期间每两周提供一次更新;详细的 监测 可在 RespTas 报告中找到;建议公众遵循活动水平指南以降低风险。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Update - acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania - Tasmanian Department of Health

News Brief
The Tasmanian Department of Health has issued an update on acute respiratory infection activity as of 19 July 2026. While overall acute respiratory illness (ARI) activity remains low but is gradually increasing, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) stands out with moderate and increasing activity, consistent with seasonal patterns. In contrast, COVID-19 and influenza activity remain low, with influenza below expected levels compared to recent years. The department updates this information fortnightly during heightened seasonal periods and directs the public to the RespTas report for detailed surveillance data. Authorities emphasize that understanding current activity levels helps individuals take informed steps to reduce transmission risks.
Original source text (English)

Update - acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania Breadcrumb Home Topics Infectious Diseases Acute Respiratory Infection Activity Update - Acute Respiratory Infection Activity In Tasmania Listen Print Main navigation Infectious diseases Infectious diseases and prevention Infectious diseases guides and factsheets Acute respiratory infections Current acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania Measles public exposure locations Rabies and Australian Bat Lyssavirus This page presents a snapshot of current acute respiratory infection activity in Tasmania. Current activity is presented for acute respiratory illness and specific notifiable viruses, these are COVID-19, influenza, and RSV. Acute respiratory illness is an overall indicator and may be due to symptoms of various infections including COVID-19, influenza, RSV, Rhinovirus and others. Activity may be presented as low, moderate or high. While monitoring by Public Health Services is continuous, this page is updated monthly during inter-seasonal periods. During increased seasonal influenza and RSV activity, this page is updated fortnightly.For detailed respiratory surveillance see RespTas report.Understanding current activity of acute respiratory infections will help you stay informed, follow activity level link to learn more about what steps you can take to reduce the risk to yourselves and others. Weekly update - acute respiratory infection activity in TasmaniaPublished: Friday 24 July 2026Data as at Sunday 19 July 2026ConditionCurrent activity Acute Respiratory IllnessLowLearn more about simple steps to follow when activity is LOWPathogen Current activityCOVID-19 LowInfluenzaLowRSVModerateWhat does this mean? As at Sunday 19 July 2026:Acute respiratory illness (ARI) activity remains low but is gradually increasing.COVID-19 activity remains low.Influenza activity remains low and below expected levels compared to recent years.Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) activity is moderate, continues to increase, and is consistent with seasonal activity. Notification rates continue to increase among children aged 0-4 years and adults aged 65 years and over.The next update will be published on Friday 7 August 2026.Published by the Health Intelligence team, Communicable Diseases Prevention Unit, Public Health Services. Sources: Tasmanian Notifiable Diseases Surveillance System; FluTracking, Hunter New England Local Health District, New South Wales Ministry of Health; DSPL — Diagnostic Services Pty Ltd; RHH — Royal Hobart Hospital Pathology.Other data sources: Notifiable disease data which includes notifiable acute respiratory infections, with breakdown by state and territory, is also published by the Commonwealth and updated daily at Dashboard · NINDSS Portal (health.gov.au)For detailed respiratory infection surveillance go to the RespTas Report pageHow you can help track ARI activity in TasmaniaYou can help Public Health to track acute respiratory illness by participating in FluTracking.FluTracking is an Australia-wide online health surveillance system operated by Hunter New England Health in NSW. It helps track the spread of COVID-19, flu and other respiratory illness. All Tasmanians are encouraged to participate, whether you’re healthy or unwell. All you need to do is complete a simple online survey each Monday, it takes less than 30 seconds. Event
Official update on acute respiratory infection activity, including confirmed moderate activity of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), reported by the Tasmanian Department of Health.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

RSV 预防剂可以预防另一种严重感染 - MedPage Today

RSV Preventive Agent May Protect Against Another Serious Infection - MedPage Today
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 27 日发表的一项研究显示,接受 RSV 预防剂 尼塞韦单抗 的法国婴儿患侵袭性肺炎球菌疾病的几率较低。该研究表明,RSV 疫苗 的潜在次要益处是预防另一种严重感染。没有提供具体的病例编号或区域详细信息,并且调查结果基于观察数据而不是 暴发 响应。该研究强调了 RSV 预防措施可能对公共卫生产生更广泛的影响,但可能需要进一步的研究来证实这些结果。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 27 日。证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
没有提及具体地区或设施。
事件
一项研究发现,接受 尼塞韦单抗(一种 RSV 预防剂)的法国婴儿患侵袭性肺炎球菌疾病的几率较低。该事件被报告为一项研究发现,而不是 暴发 或已确认的公共卫生事件。
规模、影响与风险
没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或接触数据。该研究表明,接受 尼塞韦单抗 治疗的婴儿对侵袭性肺炎球菌疾病具有潜在的保护作用。没有明确规定风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
该证据基于一项研究。没有提供有关调查、测试、隔离、接触者追踪或官方回应的信息。来源限制:内容是联合摘要,完整的原始文章未经审查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

RSV Preventive Agent May Protect Against Another Serious Infection - MedPage Today

News Brief
A study published on July 27, 2026, revealed that French babies who received nirsevimab, an RSV preventive agent, showed lower rates of invasive pneumococcal disease. The research suggests a potential secondary benefit of the RSV vaccine in protecting against another serious infection. No specific case numbers or regional details were provided, and the findings are based on observational data rather than an outbreak response. The study highlights a possible broader public health impact of RSV preventive measures, though further research may be needed to confirm these results.
Original source text (English)

A study found that French babies who received nirsevimab (an RSV preventive agent) had lower rates of invasive pneumococcal disease. The event is reported as a research finding, not an outbreak or confirmed public health incident.

No specific case counts, deaths, or exposure figures provided. The study indicates a potential protective effect against invasive pneumococcal disease in babies who received nirsevimab. No explicit risk level stated.
Response, status and uncertainty
The evidence is based on a research study. No information on investigation, testing, isolation, contact tracing, or official response provided. Source limitation: The content is a syndicated summary, and the full original article was not reviewed.

研究详细介绍了幼儿呼吸道病毒感染的全部情况

Study details full range of what respiratory virus infections look like in young kids
新闻简报《儿科学》发表的一项综合研究分析了 2019 年至 2025 年 1,223 名美国儿童的呼吸道病毒感染情况,确定了 15,898 例感染。婴儿的无症状感染率(流感高达 14.5%)和需要住院治疗的严重疾病率最高(RSV/流感 OR 2.22-4.73)。虽然 78.4% 的病例是在家中处理的,但研究结果强调了婴儿的脆弱性,并建议预防感染应包括手部卫生以外的环境清洁。该研究为儿科护理和公共卫生策略提供了重要见解。

美国;辛辛那提儿童医院医疗中心对 1,223 名从出生到 5 岁的健康儿童进行了随访。

证实了有关幼儿呼吸道病毒感染(包括 RSV、流感、hMPV 和 SARS-CoV-2)的研究结果,强调了无症状和严重疾病模式。

学习完成;作者强调,除了手卫生之外,还需要加强感染控制,包括环境改造;没有持续回应的报道。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Study details full range of what respiratory virus infections look like in young kids

News Brief
A comprehensive study published in Pediatrics analyzed respiratory virus infections in 1,223 U.S. children from 2019-2025, identifying 15,898 infections. Infants showed the highest rates of both asymptomatic infections (up to 14.5% for flu) and severe illness requiring hospital care (OR 2.22-4.73 for RSV/influenza). While 78.4% of cases were managed at home, the findings underscore the vulnerability of infants and suggest infection prevention should include environmental cleaning beyond hand hygiene. The research provides critical insights for pediatric care and public health strategies.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Main navigation News Antimicrobial Resistance COVID-19 Chronic Wasting Disease Influenza Public Health Alerts All Topics A-Z Coronavirus Vaccines Roadmap Influenza Vaccines Roadmap Roadmaps for Priority Pathogens Vaccine Integrity Project CIDRAP Leadership Forum . . Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu Study details full range of what respiratory virus infections look like in young kids Laine Bergeson Influenza, General Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) COVID-19 Jacob Wackerhausen / iStock Share Copied to clipboard Infants are far more vulnerable than older children to severe illness from viruses like influenza, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), human metapneumovirus (hMPV), and SARS-CoV-2, the virus that causes COVID-19, according to a study yesterday in Pediatrics. The findings also highlight significant differences in how respiratory infections affect infants, ranging from asymptomatic or mildly symptomatic infections managed at home to severe illness requiring hospital or emergency department (ED) care. For the study, researchers led by a team from Cincinnati Children's Hospital Medical Center followed up with 1,223 healthy children from birth to age 5 years from November 2019 to August 2025. After collecting weekly nasal swabs from participants and pairing the laboratory results with biweekly symptom surveys, the team identified 15,898 acute respiratory infections (ARIs) caused by different pathogens.Both silent and severe infectionsOverall, more than three-quarters (78.4%) of symptomatic respiratory infections were managed at home, and the remaining 21.6% were treated in a healthcare setting. The researchers identified asymptomatic infections for all respiratory pathogens, including influenza (7.6%) and RSV (10.2%). Infants younger than 1 year experienced the most asymptomatic flu and RSV infections—14.5% and 12.5%, respectively. No symptoms were reported in one-fifth of SARS-CoV-2, adenovirus, and endemic coronavirus infections or in one-third of rhinovirus and bocavirus infections.While infants experienced the highest rates of asymptomatic infections, they were simultaneously at highest risk for serious illness. Compared with older children, infants were significantly more likely to experience respiratory distress or require ED or hospital care after infection with influenza A viruses (odds ratio [OR], 4.73), RSV A (OR, 2.52), RSV B (OR, 2.22), hMPV (OR, 2.36), and SARS-CoV-2 (OR, 3.99). The study also looked at gastrointestinal symptoms. Vomiting or diarrhea accompanied about one-third of influenza infections and more than one-quarter of RSV infections, regardless of age.The authors conclude that the variation in how children respond to the same respiratory viruses could provide important clues to improving pediatric preventive and therapeutic care.Importance of environmental modifications In an accompanying commentary, Leigh M. Howard, MD, MPH, of Vanderbilt University Medical Center, and Jennifer E. Schuster, MD, of Children's Mercy Hospital in Kansas City, Missouri, note the importance of having a full accounting of viral infections in children, regardless of whether they produce symptoms. It may be important to incorporate environmental modifications (eg, air and surface cleaning) in addition to hand hygiene to complement other infection prevention measures.“Understanding the complete spectrum of disease associated with specific viral infections, including those that are asymptomatic or only mildly symptomatic, could provide important insights into estimating vaccine or nirsevimab [an RSV antibody shot] impact, community and household transmission patterns, and the socioeconomic costs of ARI that complement and enhance previous estimates largely derived from health care settings,” they write.Howard and Schuster also point out that the findings highlight the need for infection-control measures that go beyond isolation and social distancing. Because so many children with ARIs don’t have symptoms, “it may be important to incorporate environmental modifications (eg, air and surface cleaning) in addition to hand hygiene to complement other infection prevention measures.” Related news Previous Next RSV options: Most pregnant women, parents prefer the maternal RSV vaccine over the infant antibody shot Sarah Boden Many Black US patients with long COVID being missed, analysis reveals Laine Bergeson One immunization may protect newborns against 2 serious diseases Liz Szabo New study offers clues about long COVID’s brain symptoms Laine Bergeson Contagious cuteness: Children’s birthday parties spread COVID-19 to grandparents during pandemic Liz Szabo Contagious cuteness: Children’s birthday parties spread COVID-19 to grandparents during pandemic Liz Szabo Taking painkillers before flu shot doesn’t lower its effectiveness, study finds Meghan Holohan Paper highlights how nagging flu symptoms can linger for weeks Laine Bergeson This week's top reads FDA identifies new Cyclospora outbreak with unknown source The trace-back investigation for the new outbreak is underway as cases mount across the country. 解释者:RSV 是什么?为什么今年冬天它让这么多人生病? - 新西兰皇家银行

Explainer: What is RSV and why is it making so many people sick this winter? - RNZ
新闻简报呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 正在新西兰引发严重的冬季疾病,儿童和老年人的住院率很高。这种病毒通过空气飞沫传播,每年影响 1,800-2,000 名儿童,其中婴儿和 1 岁以下儿童尤其容易受到感染。毛利人和太平洋岛民儿童面临的风险要高得多。虽然 RSV 通常会在健康个体中引起类似感冒的症状,但它也可能导致肺炎和 毛细支气管炎 等严重并发症,尤其是在免疫功能低下的个体中。卫生专家主张批准已在其他国家上市的 RSV 疫苗,并引用其在减少住院治疗方面有效的证据。目前,新西兰不存在全民儿童 疫苗,治疗 仍然是支持性护理。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日。RSV 活动正在上升至高水平,通常在 5 月至 10 月的冬季达到顶峰。
地点与对象
新西兰,重病发病率很高。受影响人群包括儿童(尤其是 6 个月以下和 1 岁以下的婴儿)、老年人(65 岁以上)以及免疫力低下或患有慢性健康问题的人群。毛利人和太平洋岛民儿童住院的可能性是其他人的 3-5 倍。
事件
关于 RSV (呼吸道合胞病毒) 在新西兰造成严重冬季疾病的解释文章。健康专家呼吁实施全国儿童 疫苗接种 计划,理由是住院率较高,而且其他国家也有 疫苗 的可用性。
规模、影响与风险
每年约有 1,800-2,000 名儿童因 RSV 住院,其中一半是婴儿。成年人,尤其是 65 岁以上或患有慢性病的成年人,面临严重的并发症,包括住院治疗,死亡率 率为 4-8%。目前,RSV 引起的病例比 COVID-19 或流感还要多。
应对、状态与不确定性
不存在 抗病毒药物 治疗;管理重点是支持性护理。单克隆抗体(帕利珠单抗)可用于高危婴儿,但尽管专家呼吁,新西兰尚未批准通用儿童 疫苗。疫苗 在美国、英国和澳大利亚上市,有证据表明它在减少住院治疗方面取得了成功。公共卫生传播中心已敦促政府批准并资助这些 疫苗。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Explainer: What is RSV and why is it making so many people sick this winter? - RNZ

News Brief
Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is causing significant winter illness in New Zealand, with high hospitalization rates among children and older adults. The virus, which spreads through airborne droplets, affects 1,800-2,000 children annually, with infants and those under 1 being particularly vulnerable. Māori and Pasifika children face disproportionately higher risks. While RSV typically causes cold-like symptoms in healthy individuals, it can lead to severe complications like pneumonia and bronchiolitis, especially in immunocompromised individuals. Health experts are advocating for the approval of RSV vaccines already available in other countries, citing evidence of their effectiveness in reducing hospitalizations. Currently, no universal childhood vaccine exists in New Zealand, and treatment remains supportive care.
Original source text (English)
24 July 2026, 7:30am Explainer: What is RSV and why is it making so many people sick this winter? Nik Dirga Digital Explainer Editor Caption: Three-dimensional renditions of the 'troublesome three' respiratory viruses: Covid-19 (left), RSV (centre), and influenza (right). Photo credit: NIH-NIAID / IMAGE POINT FR / AFP Explainer: RSV may not quite have the name recognition that influenza and Covid do, but it's actually causing more winter illness. New Zealand has some of the world's highest rates of serious illness caused by the virus, and health experts are calling for a national childhood vaccination programme to be instituted. The Public Health Communication Centre issued a briefing this week asking for the government to approve RSV vaccines being used in Australia and other countries, which it says will also relieve the enormous burden put on doctors and hospitals each winter. "It's an awful experience for families to see their children struggling to breathe," Emma Best, an associate professor of paediatric and youth health at Auckland University, told RNZ's Morning Report . What exactly is RSV? Dr Hasan Bhally is an infectious diseases specialist Supplied Respiratory syncytial virus - RSV for short - is exceedingly common, particularly in the winter months from May to October. It causes infections in the lung and respiratory tract. For some people it'll have symptoms similar to the common cold and fade after 10 days or so. But for children and older people with compromised immunity, it can be much more serious. Dr Hasan Bhally, an infectious disease doctor at North Shore Hospital, told RNZ's Nine To Noon that RSV is actually causing more cases than Covid-19 or influenza according to recent data. "RSV is the most common of those troublesome three with about 45 percent, versus 25 percent for Covid and 30 percent for influenza." Winter ills and RSV vaccinations Nine To Noon 24 June 2026 "When you look at the viruses being seen over the last weeks amongst hospitalised people, RSV is much more than flu at the moment," Best added. RSV spreads through the air when people sneeze or cough. People are typically infectious for about 10 days after symptoms have started. There's plenty of chatter on social media about people suffering long illnesses lately, and much of that could be attributed to RSV, Best said. "With RSV peaking at the moment it is likely the cause of the comment 'a lot of people talking about being sick recently,'" she noted. The Respiratory Illness Dashboard run by PHF Science shows a steady rise in illnesses typically reported through July and into September. Currently trends are "rising toward high so not at peak winter illnesses yet," Best said. Is it worse for children? Around 1800 to 2000 children every year are hospitalised with RSV, the Public Health Communication Centre report said. About half of those are infants, particularly those under 6 months. "Beyond the immediate illness, there is increasing evidence that RSV infection in infancy is associated with recurrent preschool wheeze and asthma, further adding to the long-term burden on families and the health system," the report noted. RSV so far isn't actually worse than in previous years, but it is rising at typical levels for winter. "It is predictably high at this time of year and leading to children under 5 and particularly under 1 being hospitalised," Best said. Māori and Pasifika children are three to five times more likely to be hospitalised, the Cardiac and Respiratory Foundation NZ says. Thousands of babies born each year "are healthy but acquire RSV, have an awful wheezing illness, they need oxygen, they need support with their feeding, they need hospitalisation essentially," Best said. "It's really predictable for us, we see RSV coming, beds are full in hospitals." But adults can get it too? Definitely, and it can cause life-threatening complications. "It is true that it's predominantly a virus of babies, especially infants, but there is a significant burden in adults also, especially the older adults, which I will define as 65 years and more," Bhally said. "Our immunity wanes from natural infections as we all age," he explained. Adults who have chronic health problems like asthma are also highly at risk, he said. "Infections in these people can be quite severe, meaning it can lead to hospitalisation." Babies are particularly affected by RSV. 123RF How is it treated? "There's unfortunately no antiviral treatment for RSV," Bhally said. Because it's a viral infection, not bacterial, antibiotics don't work on RSV. For adults the general recommendations is to isolate yourself, drink fluids, rest and take painkillers as needed. For children, it's important to monitor their health closely - infants may not show symptoms as obviously as adults. A medicine called palivizumab is available for some infants and children who are at high risk of severe illness, Health NZ says. Palivizumab is given by injections repeated once a month during the RSV season. A healthcare provider can determine if a child is eligible. Anyone who shows signs of trouble breathing should seek immediate medical attention, as complications like pneumonia or bronchiolitis can develop. Is there a vaccine? There's no universal childhood vaccine programme in New Zealand, although there are growing calls to start one. There are childhood vaccines already available in the US, UK, and Australia, but they have not yet been approved for use in New Zealand yet, a fact which has angered medical experts . Paediatricians push for funded RSV vaccine Morning Report 22 July 2026 "We just feel disappointed really, because it's been almost two years now in Australia that they've had some prevention for RSV," Best said. In Australia, the ABC reported New South Wales are hailing the vaccination program launched in 2024 that's nearly halved hospitalisations ahead of the winter peak as a "fantastic" success. Vaccines for RSV are relatively recent developments - there are long-acting monoclonal antibodies given to newborns, and maternal vaccination given during pregnancy. "The landscape of RSV vaccinations is changing rapidly," Bhally said. "It's very exciting times if we look at what's happening globally with RSV vaccinations." "We have to make the vaccines available, funded and accessible," he said. Both Best and Dr Bhally called upon the government to approve and fund them for use in New Zealand. "These have been licensed in Europe, US and Australia for several years already, so again we don't know why these things take longer when we've already got international agencies that have licensed them," Best said. There is also a vaccine aimed at adults over 60 or those between 50 to 59 who are at increased risk, called Arexvy. However, it's not funded by Pharmac and runs more than $400. "RSV is unfortunately, one of those viruses that in older adults it does have a mortality ranging from 4 to 8 percent, so it is a serious disease," Bhally said. 24 July 2026, 7:30am
View five news elements
Time
Published on 24 July 2026. RSV activity is rising toward high levels, typically peaking in winter months from May to October.
Location and population
New Zealand, with high rates of serious illness. Affected populations include children (especially infants under 6 months and under 1 year), older adults (65+), and those with compromised immunity or chronic health conditions. Māori and Pasifika children are 3-5 times more likely to be hospitalized.
Event
An explainer article about RSV (respiratory syncytial virus) causing significant winter illness in New Zealand. Health experts are calling for a national childhood vaccination program, citing high hospitalization rates and the availability of vaccines in other countries.
Scale, impact and risk
Approximately 1,800-2,000 children are hospitalized annually with RSV, half of whom are infants. Adults, especially those 65+ or with chronic conditions, face severe complications including hospitalization and mortality rates of 4-8%. RSV is currently causing more cases than COVID-19 or influenza.
Response, status and uncertainty
No antiviral treatment exists; management focuses on supportive care. A monoclonal antibody (palivizumab) is available for high-risk infants, but no universal childhood vaccine is approved in New Zealand despite calls from experts. Vaccines are available in the US, UK, and Australia, with evidence showing success in reducing hospitalizations. The Public Health Communication Centre has urged the government to approve and fund these vaccines.

Clesrovimab 在高风险婴儿的第二个 RSV 季节中证明了安全性 - AJMC

Clesrovimab Demonstrates Safety Through a Second RSV Season in High-Risk Infants - AJMC
新闻简报最近发表在 JAMA Pediatrics 上的一项 3 期研究表明,clesrovimab(Enflonsia;Merck)在高危婴儿的第二个 RSV 季节中仍然具有良好的耐受性。该试验在 27 个国家的 110 个地点进行,涉及 1003 名婴儿,其中 276 名婴儿在第二季接受了 210 毫克剂量。clesrovimab 组和帕利珠单抗组之间的不良事件相当(第 1 季为 75.3% vs 79.6%;第 2 季为 66.7% vs 68.1%),其中大多数为轻度至中度。该研究支持 clesrovimab 作为单剂量 RSV 预防药物的安全性,尽管其局限性包括第二季没有比较器以及统计功效降低。研究结果强调了在这一弱势群体中提高单剂量治疗方案依从性的潜力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2021年11月30日至2025年11月20日期间进行,结果于2026年7月20日在线发布,并于2026年7月23日访问。
地点与对象
这项 3 期随机试验在 27 个国家和地区的 110 个地点进行,涉及 1003 名患有严重 RSV 疾病风险较高的婴儿,包括患有慢性肺病和先天性心脏病的婴儿。
事件
Clesrovimab 在高危婴儿的第二个 RSV 季节中表现出安全性和耐受性,210 mg 剂量显示与第一个季节的帕利珠单抗相当的不良事件特征。
规模、影响与风险
In RSV season 1, 498 infants received 105 mg of clesrovimab and 499 received palivizumab; in season 2, 276 infants received 210 mg of clesrovimab. Adverse events were comparable between groups (75.3% vs 79.6% in season 1; 66.7% vs 68.1% in season 2), with most being grade 1 or 2.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究通过两个RSV季节证实了clesrovimab的安全性,但存在局限性:季节2缺乏对照组,且样本量不足以进行非劣效性评估。单次给药方案被认为可能提高这一脆弱人群的依从性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Clesrovimab Demonstrates Safety Through a Second RSV Season in High-Risk Infants - AJMC

News Brief
A recent phase 3 study published in JAMA Pediatrics demonstrates that clesrovimab (Enflonsia; Merck) remains well tolerated through a second RSV season in high-risk infants. Conducted across 110 sites in 27 countries, the trial involved 1003 infants, with 276 receiving a 210-mg dose in the second season. Adverse events were comparable between clesrovimab and palivizumab groups (75.3% vs 79.6% in season 1; 66.7% vs 68.1% in season 2), with most being mild to moderate. The study supports clesrovimab's safety as a single-dose RSV prevention, though limitations included no comparator in season 2 and reduced statistical power. The findings highlight the potential for improved adherence with a single-dose regimen in this vulnerable population.
Original source text (English)
Clesrovimab remained well tolerated through a second RSV season in infants at increased risk of severe RSV. Clesrovimab (Enflonsia; Merck) was well tolerated in infants with an increased risk of severe respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) disease through their 2nd RSV season, according to a recent study published in JAMA Pediatrics . 1 Clesrovimab is a monoclonal antibody approved as a single fixed dose for neonates and infants born during or entering their first RSV season . The monoclonal antibody was added to the growing landscape of long-acting monoclonal antibodies preventing RSV in June 2025. 2 Although proven efficacious through neonates’ and infants’ first RSV season, there is limited data on its safety and tolerability for children who are at high risk for severe RSV disease. The study reported the end-of-study results of the SMART ( NCT04938830 ) through 2 RSV seasons. 1 The phase 3 randomized, partially masked, active comparator-controlled trial was conducted between November 30, 2021, and November 20, 2025, across 110 sites in 27 countries and territories. Participants were infants recommended for palivizumab (Synagis; MedImmune), another long-acting monoclonal antibody, as standard of care prevention for RSV in high-risk infants. Clesrovimab was well tolerated through a second RSV season in high-risk infants, supporting its safety as a single-dose RSV prevention. | Image credit: zilvergolf - stock.adobe.com In RSV season 1, participants were randomized 1:1 to receive 105 mg of clesrovimab or 15 mg/kg body weight of palivizumab. On day 28, participants in the palivizumab group received a second dose, while those in the clesrovimab group received a placebo. In the open-label second-season phase, participants received 210 mg of clesrovimab 4 weeks before RSV season 2—aligned with Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices (ACIP) recommendations for nirsevimab in high-risk children entering their second RSV season. 3 Infants at increased risk of severe RSV disease include those with comorbidities such as chronic lung disease (CLD) and congenital heart disease (CHD), among other certain conditions. ACIP recommendations note that older infants have greater body weight and require higher antibody exposure to maintain protective concentrations, consistent with second-season dosing principles used for other long-acting RSV monoclonal antibodies. The study’s primary outcomes were safety and tolerability, specifically any participants who experienced solicited adverse events (AEs), and solicited daily body temperature to assess fever. Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were also evaluated. They included anaphylaxis and/or hypersensitivity and rash through 42 days after the dose and other nonserious AEs and serious AEs throughout season 1. Secondary outcomes evaluate incidence of RSV-associated disease outcomes like RSV-associated medically attended lower respiratory infection or severity and incidence of RSV-associated hospitalization. There were 1003 participants enrolled in RSV season 1, of whom 997 received study treatment. Of them, 498 received 105 mg of clesrovimab, and 499 received palivizumab. The median age was 2.6 months, and the median weight was 3.3 kg. In RSV season 2, there were 276 patients who received 210 mg of clesrovimab. The median age at dosing was 14.4 months, and the median weight was 8.4 kg. Researchers noted the proportion of participants with adverse events in RSV season 2 was comparable between those who received clesrovimab or palivizumab in RSV season 1 (92 of 138 [66.7%] with ≥1 AE among participants who received clesrovimab vs 94 of 138 [68.1%] among participants who received palivizumab). There were 374 of 497 [75.3%] with ≥ 1 AE in the clesrovimab 105 mg group when compared with 397 of 499 [79.6%] in the palivizumab group in RSV season 1. Solicited injection-site AEs were observed among 18 of 276 children (6.5%) receiving a 210-mg dose (administered as two 105-mg injections) in season 2, the most common being injection-site pain. The majority of AEs were grade 1 or 2. Rash AESI was reportedly low in both seasons (3 [0.6%] in the clesrovimab group and 1 [0.2%] in the palivizumab group in season 1; 3 [1.1%] after 210 mg of clesrovimab in season 2; all grade 1). “The safety profile of a 210-mg second season dose of clesrovimab was comparable between children receiving clesrovimab over 2 consecutive RSV seasons and those who had received palivizumab in their first RSV season,” the study authors wrote. The study was limited by the absence of a comparator arm in season 2. Additionally, participant sample size limited the study’s power to assess noninferiority of clesrovimab when compared with palivizumab. “The single-dose regimen reduces the number of injections, potentially improving adherence, an important advantage for this vulnerable population,” the study authors concluded. 1. Zar HJ, Bont LJ, Manzoni P, et al. Clesrovimab in infants at increased risk for severe disease during RSV seasons. JAMA Pediatr. Published online July 20, 2026. doi:10.1001/jamapediatrics.2026.2760 2. US FDA approves Merck’s ENFLONSIATM (Clesrovimab-CFOR) for prevention of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) lower respiratory tract disease in infants born during or entering their first RSV season. ACIP evidence to recommendations for use of Nirsevimab in children 8–19 months of age at increased risk of severe disease entering their second RSV season | ACIP | CDC. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. September 5, 2024. Accessed July 23, 2026. https://www.cdc.gov/acip/evidence-to-recommendations/nirsevimab-season2-rsv-infants-children-etr.html
View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted between November 30, 2021, and November 20, 2025, with results published online July 20, 2026, and accessed July 23, 2026.
Location and population
The phase 3 randomized trial was conducted across 110 sites in 27 countries and territories, involving 1003 infants at increased risk of severe RSV disease, including those with chronic lung disease and congenital heart disease.
Event
Clesrovimab demonstrated safety and tolerability through a second RSV season in high-risk infants, with a 210-mg dose showing comparable adverse event profiles to palivizumab in the first season.
Scale, impact and risk
In RSV season 1, 498 infants received 105 mg of clesrovimab and 499 received palivizumab; in season 2, 276 infants received 210 mg of clesrovimab. Adverse events were comparable between groups (75.3% vs 79.6% in season 1; 66.7% vs 68.1% in season 2), with most being grade 1 or 2.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study confirmed clesrovimab's safety through two RSV seasons but was limited by the absence of a comparator arm in season 2 and a smaller sample size for noninferiority assessment. The single-dose regimen was noted as potentially improving adherence in this vulnerable population.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

JAMA 研究发现 Clesrovimab 在两个季节预防 RSV 方面具有良好的耐受性

Clesrovimab Well Tolerated for RSV Prevention Through Two Seasons, Finds JAMA Study
新闻简报JAMA 儿科研究证实,clesrovimab(一种长效单克隆抗体)在两个 RSV 季节的高危婴儿中具有良好的耐受性。3 期 SMART 试验招募了 27 个国家的 997 名婴儿,比较第一季的单次 clesrovimab 剂量与每月一次的帕利珠单抗,以及第二季的更高 210 毫克剂量。组间不良事件发生率相当,RSV 感染率相似。虽然单剂量方案具有潜在的依从性益处,但其局限性包括没有第二季比较物以及证明非劣效性的能力不足。研究结果支持 clesrovimab 在持续存在风险的婴儿中延长使用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-26。该研究评估了两个 RSV 季节的数据,第一季的分析截至第 150 天,第二季的分析截至第 180 天。
地点与对象
该研究在 27 个国家和地区进行,来自 110 个地点的 997 名高危婴儿入组。参与者是患有严重 RSV 疾病风险较高的婴儿,包括早产儿或患有慢性肺病或先天性心脏病的婴儿。
事件
发表在《JAMA Pediatrics》上的一项 3 期随机临床试验(SMART 试验)发现,clesrovimab(一种长效单克隆抗体)在高危婴儿的两个 RSV 季节中对 RSV 预防具有良好的耐受性。该研究在第一季中比较了单剂量的 clesrovimab 与每月一次的帕利珠单抗,并在第二季中评估了更高的 210 毫克剂量。
规模、影响与风险
在 997 名婴儿中,第一季期间,clesrovimab 和 palivizumab 组的不良事件发生率相当。RSV 相关就医下呼吸道感染 (MALRI) 的 发病率 相似(3.2% vs 3.4%)。在第二个季节,210 mg 剂量下 RSV 相关 MALRI 的累积 发病率 为 7.3%。由于功效有限,该研究并未确定相对于帕利珠单抗的非劣效性。
应对、状态与不确定性
该试验证实了 clesrovimab 在两个季节的安全性和耐受性,但也指出了其局限性,包括第二个季节缺乏比较组、未经调整的安全性比较以及缺乏长期呼吸结果数据。作者得出的结论是,与每月注射相比,单剂量方案可以简化预防并提高依从性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Clesrovimab Well Tolerated for RSV Prevention Through Two Seasons, Finds JAMA Study

News Brief
A JAMA Pediatrics study confirms that clesrovimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody, is well tolerated for RSV prevention in high-risk infants across two RSV seasons. The phase 3 SMART trial enrolled 997 infants across 27 countries, comparing a single clesrovimab dose to monthly palivizumab in the first season and a higher 210-mg dose in the second. Adverse event rates were comparable, and RSV infection rates were similar between groups. While the single-dose regimen offers potential adherence benefits, limitations included no second-season comparator and insufficient power to prove noninferiority. The findings support clesrovimab's extended use in infants at continued risk.
Original source text (English)

and Neonatology NewsClesrovimab Well...Clesrovimab Well Tolerated for RSV Prevention Through Two Seasons, Finds JAMA Study Written By : Medha Baranwal |Medically Reviewed By : Dr. Kamal Kant Kohli Published On 2026-07-26T21:00:25+05:30 | Updated On 26 July 2026 9:00 PM ISTUSA: Researchers have found in a randomized clinical trial that clesrovimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody, was well tolerated in infants at increased risk of severe respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) disease across two RSV seasons. The findings support its potential use in children who continue to be at elevated risk for severe RSV disease during their second RSV season.The phase 3 trial, published in JAMA Pediatrics, was led by Heather J. Zar from the Department of Paediatrics & Child Health and the South African Medical Research Council Unit on Child & Adolescent Health at the University of Cape Town, South Africa, along with colleagues. The study evaluated the safety and tolerability of a single dose of clesrovimab compared with monthly palivizumab during the first RSV season and assessed the safety of a higher 210-mg dose of clesrovimab in children who remained at increased risk during a second RSV season. Respiratory syncytial virus is a major cause of lower respiratory tract infections in infants, especially those born prematurely or with chronic lung disease or congenital heart disease. Although clesrovimab is approved for preventing RSV disease during the first RSV season, data on its use in subsequent seasons have been limited.To address this, the phase 3 SMART (MK-1654-007) trial enrolled 997 high-risk infants from 110 sites across 27 countries and territories. Participants were randomized to receive either a single 105-mg dose of clesrovimab followed by placebo or monthly palivizumab for up to five doses during the first RSV season. Children who remained at high risk received an open-label 210-mg dose of clesrovimab before their second RSV season.Key findings included:Among 997 treated infants, adverse event rates during the first RSV season were comparable between those receiving single-dose clesrovimab (498 infants) and monthly palivizumab (499 infants).A 210-mg dose of clesrovimab administered to 276 children before their second RSV season was generally well tolerated.The incidence of RSV-associated medically attended lower respiratory infection (MALRI) through day 150 of the first season was similar between the clesrovimab and palivizumab groups (3.2% vs 3.4%, respectively).Following the 210-mg dose in the second RSV season, the cumulative incidence of RSV-associated MALRI through day 180 was 7.3%.Pharmacokinetic findings for both the 105-mg and 210-mg doses were consistent with those previously observed in healthy infants in whom efficacy had already been established.The authors acknowledged several limitations, including the absence of a comparator group during the second RSV season, limited power to establish noninferiority versus palivizumab, unadjusted safety comparisons for multiple testing, and the lack of assessment of long-term respiratory outcomes.The researchers concluded that clesrovimab was well tolerated across two RSV seasons in infants at increased risk of severe RSV disease. They noted that its single-dose regimen may simplify RSV prevention and improve treatment adherence compared with monthly palivizumab injections.Reference:Zar HJ, Bont LJ, Manzoni P, et al. Clesrovimab in Infants at Increased Risk For Severe Disease During 2 RSV Seasons: A Randomized Clinical Trial. JAMA Pediatr. View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-07-26. The study evaluated data across two RSV seasons, with the first season's analysis through day 150 and the second season's through day 180.
Location and population
Conducted across 27 countries and territories with 997 high-risk infants enrolled from 110 sites. Participants were infants at increased risk of severe RSV disease, including those born prematurely or with chronic lung disease or congenital heart disease.
Event
A phase 3 randomized clinical trial (SMART trial) published in JAMA Pediatrics found that clesrovimab, a long-acting monoclonal antibody, was well tolerated for RSV prevention through two RSV seasons in high-risk infants. The study compared a single dose of clesrovimab with monthly palivizumab during the first season and assessed a higher 210-mg dose in the second season.
Scale, impact and risk
Among 997 infants, adverse event rates were comparable between clesrovimab and palivizumab groups during the first season. The incidence of RSV-associated medically attended lower respiratory infection (MALRI) was similar (3.2% vs 3.4%). In the second season, the cumulative incidence of RSV-associated MALRI with the 210-mg dose was 7.3%. The study did not establish noninferiority versus palivizumab due to limited power.
Response, status and uncertainty
The trial confirmed clesrovimab's safety and tolerability across two seasons but noted limitations, including the absence of a comparator group in the second season, unadjusted safety comparisons, and lack of long-term respiratory outcome data. The authors concluded that the single-dose regimen may simplify prevention and improve adherence compared to monthly injections.

不,游轮不是漂浮的培养皿 - 科普

No, cruise ships aren’t floating petri dishes - Popular Science
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 24 日的一篇《大众科学》文章研究了游轮上的 传播 疾病风险,特别针对 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 以及其他疾病。这篇文章阐述了传染病专家 William Schaffner 博士和 Freedman 博士的见解,澄清说,虽然游轮由于长时间的密切接触确实会带来更高的 传播 风险,但 RSV 和其他呼吸道感染对大多数乘客来说通常是轻微的。这篇文章指出,呼吸道合胞病毒在乘客和船员中传播,约占向船舶医疗队报告的疾病的三分之一。文章承认老年旅客和免疫系统受损的旅客面临的风险增加,但同时强调邮轮公司已经实施了有效的预防措施,近年来胃肠道疾病发生率降低了 50%。该文章还将最近的汉坦病毒 暴发 视为异常现象,而不是典型的游轮风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日。证据中未报告具体事件日期。
地点与对象
全球范围内的游轮,特别提到 2026 年 5 月 18 日在鹿特丹港停泊的 MV Hondius。受影响的人群包括乘客和船员。
事件
呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV) 与流感等其他呼吸道感染一起在游轮乘客和船员中传播。该文章讨论了游轮上的疾病 传播 风险,指出 RSV 是这种情况下咳嗽和感冒的常见原因。
规模、影响与风险
没有提供 RSV 的具体病例数。文章指出,向船舶医疗队报告的疾病中大约三分之一是呼吸道感染。由于长时间的密切接触,游轮上的风险更高,与飞行等短途旅行相比,较长航程的暴露风险显着增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章指出,邮轮公司已实施预防措施,导致 2006 年至 2019 年间胃肠道疾病发病率降低了 50%。然而,呼吸道疾病的确切风险数据有限,因为它只反映了足以到达医疗团队的严重病例,许多轻症病例没有报告。来源是《大众科学》的完整原始报告。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

No, cruise ships aren’t floating petri dishes - Popular Science

News Brief
A July 24, 2026 Popular Science article examines disease transmission risks on cruise ships, specifically addressing respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) alongside other illnesses. The article, featuring insights from infectious disease experts Dr. William Schaffner and Dr. Freedman, clarifies that while cruise ships do pose higher transmission risks due to prolonged close contact, RSV and other respiratory infections are generally mild for most passengers. The piece notes that RSV spreads among passengers and crew, representing about a third of illnesses reported to ship medical teams. While acknowledging increased risks for older travelers and those with compromised immune systems, the article emphasizes that cruise lines have implemented effective prevention measures, evidenced by a 50% reduction in gastrointestinal illness rates over recent years. The article also contextualizes recent hantavirus outbreaks as anomalies rather than typical cruise ship risks.
Original source text (English)
